### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For an electromagnetic wave propagating through vacuum, $\vec{k}$, $\vec{E}$ and $\omega$ represent propagation vector, electric field and angular frequency, respectively. The magnetic field associated with this wave is represented by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{\vec{E} \times \vec{k}}{\omega}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{\vec{k} \times \vec{E}}{\omega}$
- **(C)** $\omega(\vec{E} \times \vec{k})$
- **(D)** $\omega(\vec{k} \times \vec{E})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{\vec{k} \times \vec{E}}{\omega}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an electromagnetic wave, the direction of wave propagation $\hat{k}$ is along the direction of $\vec{E} \times \vec{B}$.

Since $\vec{E}$, $\vec{B}$, and $\vec{k}$ form a mutually perpendicular right-handed system, the direction of the magnetic field is given by $\hat{B} = \hat{k} \times \hat{E}$.

The magnitudes of the electric and magnetic fields are related by $B = \dfrac{E}{c}$.

Using the relation for the speed of light $c = \dfrac{\omega}{k}$, we get $B = \dfrac{kE}{\omega}$.

Combining the magnitude and direction gives:

$\vec{B} = B\hat{B} = \dfrac{kE}{\omega} (\hat{k} \times \hat{E})$

Since $\vec{k} = k\hat{k}$ and $\vec{E} = E\hat{E}$, this simplifies to:

$\vec{B} = \dfrac{\vec{k} \times \vec{E}}{\omega}$

Answer: $\dfrac{\vec{k} \times \vec{E}}{\omega}$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A point light source emits E.M. waves in free space. A detector, placed at a distance of $L$ m, measures the intensity as $I_o$. The detector is now shifted to another location on the same spherical surface ensuring the angle between original location and new location as $45°$. The measured intensity at new location will be _______.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{I_o}{4}$
- **(B)** $I_o$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{I_o}{\sqrt{2}}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{I_o}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $I_o$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a point light source, the intensity of electromagnetic waves at a distance $r$ is given by $I = \dfrac{P}{4\pi r^2}$, where $P$ is the power of the source.

Since the detector is moved to a new location on the same spherical surface, the distance from the source remains $L$.

The emission from a point source is isotropic, meaning the intensity depends only on the radial distance from the source and is independent of the angular position.

Therefore, the intensity at the new location remains unchanged, which is $I_o$.

Answer: $I_o$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A displacement current of $4.0$ A can be set up in the space between two parallel plates of $6$ $\mu$F capacitor. The rate of change of potential difference across the plates of the capacitor is nearly $\alpha \times 10^6$ V/s. The value of $\alpha$ is __________.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.58$
- **(B)** $0.67$
- **(C)** $0.82$
- **(D)** $0.75$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.67$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The displacement current $I_d$ between the plates of a capacitor is given by the formula:

$I_d = C \dfrac{dV}{dt}$

Substituting the given values of displacement current and capacitance:

$4.0 = 6 \times 10^{-6} \times \dfrac{dV}{dt}$

$\dfrac{dV}{dt} = \dfrac{4.0}{6 \times 10^{-6}}$

$\dfrac{dV}{dt} = \dfrac{2}{3} \times 10^6 \approx 0.67 \times 10^6$ V/s

Comparing this with the given rate of change of potential difference $\alpha \times 10^6$ V/s, we get $\alpha = 0.67$.

Answer: $0.67$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A magnetic field vector in an electromagnetic wave is represented by $\vec{B}=B_0\sin\left(2\pi vt-\dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right)\hat{j}$. Its associated electric field vector is ______.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{E}=-v\lambda B_0\sin\left(2\pi vt-\dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right)\hat{k}$
- **(B)** $\vec{E}=-v\lambda B_0\sin\left(2\pi vt-\dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right)\hat{i}$
- **(C)** $\vec{E}=v\lambda B_0\sin\left(2\pi vt-\dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right)\hat{k}$
- **(D)** $\vec{E}=v\lambda B_0\sin\left(2\pi vt-\dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right)\hat{i}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\vec{E}=-v\lambda B_0\sin\left(2\pi vt-\dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right)\hat{k}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction of propagation of the electromagnetic wave is determined by the phase term $2\pi vt - \dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}$, which indicates that the wave is travelling along the $+x$ direction ($\hat{i}$).

The direction of the magnetic field $\vec{B}$ is given as $\hat{j}$.

In an electromagnetic wave, the direction of propagation is given by the direction of the cross product $\vec{E} \times \vec{B}$.

Let the direction of the electric field $\vec{E}$ be $\hat{n}$.

$\hat{n} \times \hat{j} = \hat{i}$

Since $(-\hat{k}) \times \hat{j} = \hat{i}$, the direction of the electric field must be $-\hat{k}$.

The amplitude of the electric field is related to the magnetic field by $E_0 = c B_0$, where $c$ is the speed of the wave.

The speed of the wave is $c = v \lambda$, where $v$ is the frequency.

$E_0 = v \lambda B_0$

Therefore, the electric field vector is $\vec{E} = -v \lambda B_0 \sin\left(2\pi vt - \dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right) \hat{k}$.

Answer: $\vec{E}=-v\lambda B_0\sin\left(2\pi vt-\dfrac{2\pi x}{\lambda}\right)\hat{k}$

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An electromagnetic wave travels in free space along the $x$-direction. At a particular point in space and time, $\vec{B} = 2 \times 10^{-7} \hat{j}$ T is associated with this wave. The value of corresponding electric field $\vec{E}$ at this point is _______ V/m.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $60 \hat{k}$
- **(B)** $-60 \hat{k}$
- **(C)** $30 \hat{k}$
- **(D)** $-600 \hat{k}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-60 \hat{k}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction of propagation of an electromagnetic wave is given by the direction of $\vec{E} \times \vec{B}$.

Given that the wave travels along the $x$-direction, the direction of propagation is $\hat{i}$.

The magnetic field is given as $\vec{B} = 2 \times 10^{-7} \hat{j}$ T, so its direction is $\hat{j}$.

Let the direction of the electric field be $\hat{n}$. We must have:

$\hat{n} \times \hat{j} = \hat{i}$

Since $(-\hat{k}) \times \hat{j} = \hat{i}$, the direction of the electric field is $-\hat{k}$.

The magnitude of the electric field is related to the magnetic field by $E = cB$, where $c = 3 \times 10^8$ m/s is the speed of light in free space.

$E = (3 \times 10^8) \times (2 \times 10^{-7}) = 60$ V/m

Therefore, the electric field vector is $\vec{E} = -60 \hat{k}$ V/m.

Answer: $-60 \hat{k}$

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An electromagnetic wave travelling in $x$-direction is described by field equation $E_y = 300\sin\omega\left(t - \dfrac{x}{c}\right)$. If the electron is restricted to move in $y$-direction only with speed of $1.5 \times 10^6$ m/s then ratio of maximum electric and magnetic forces acting on the electron is _______.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $200$
- **(B)** $150$
- **(C)** $400$
- **(D)** $300$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $200$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The maximum electric force acting on the electron is $F_e = eE_0$.

The maximum magnetic force acting on the electron is $F_m = evB_0$, where $v$ is the speed of the electron and $B_0$ is the maximum magnetic field.

The ratio of the maximum electric force to the maximum magnetic force is given by $\dfrac{F_e}{F_m} = \dfrac{eE_0}{evB_0} = \dfrac{E_0}{vB_0}$.

For an electromagnetic wave, the ratio of the amplitudes of the electric and magnetic fields is $\dfrac{E_0}{B_0} = c$, where $c$ is the speed of light in vacuum.

Substituting this into the ratio of forces yields $\dfrac{F_e}{F_m} = \dfrac{c}{v}$.

Given $c = 3 \times 10^8$ m/s and $v = 1.5 \times 10^6$ m/s, we get $\dfrac{F_e}{F_m} = \dfrac{3 \times 10^8}{1.5 \times 10^6} = 200$.

Answer: $200$

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane electromagnetic wave is moving in free space with velocity $c=3 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$ and its electric field is given as $\vec{E}=54 \sin (k z-\omega t) \hat{j} \mathrm{~V} / \mathrm{m}$, where $\hat{j}$ is the unit vector along $y$-axis. The magnetic field vector $\vec{B}$ of the wave is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+1.8 \times 10^{-7} \sin (k z-\omega t) \hat{i} \mathrm{~T}$
- **(B)** $1.4 \times 10^{-7} \sin (k z-\omega t) \hat{k} \mathrm{~T}$
- **(C)** $-1.8 \times 10^{-7} \sin (k z-\omega t) \hat{i} \mathrm{~T}$
- **(D)** $1.4 \times 10^{-7} \sin (k z-\omega t) \hat{i} \mathrm{~T}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $-1.8 \times 10^{-7} \sin (k z-\omega t) \hat{i} \mathrm{~T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an electromagnetic wave, the relationship between electric and magnetic field amplitudes is $B_0 = \frac{E_0}{c}$.
Here $E_0 = 54$ V/m and $c = 3 \times 10^8$ m/s.
So, $B_0 = \frac{54}{3 \times 10^8} = 1.8 \times 10^{-7}$ T.
The electric field is along the $\hat{j}$ direction.
Since the wave travels in the $+z$ direction, the magnetic field direction is determined by $\vec{E} \times \vec{B}$ pointing in the $+z$ direction.
Using the right-hand rule: $\hat{j} \times (-\hat{i}) = \hat{k}$ (positive $z$ direction), confirming the magnetic field is in the $-\hat{i}$ direction.
Therefore, $\vec{B} = -1.8 \times 10^{-7}\sin(kz - \omega t)\hat{i}$ T.

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field of an electromagnetic wave travelling through a medium is given by 
 $\vec{E}(x, t)=25 \sin \left(2.0 \times 10^{15} t-10^{7} x\right) \hat{n}$ 
 then the refractive index of the medium is $\_\_\_\_$. 
 (All given measurement are in SI units)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.5
- **(B)** 1.2
- **(C)** 1.7
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 1.5**

**Detailed Solution:**

The electric field is $E(x,t) = 25\sin(2.0 \times 10^{15}t - 10^7 x)$ V/m.
This is in the standard form $E = E_0\sin(\omega t - kx)$ where angular frequency $\omega = 2.0 \times 10^{15}$ rad/s and wave number $k = 10^7$ rad/m.
The speed of the wave in the medium is $v = \frac{\omega}{k} = \frac{2.0 \times 10^{15}}{10^7} = 2.0 \times 10^8$ m/s.
The refractive index is defined as $n = \frac{c}{v}$ where $c = 3.0 \times 10^8$ m/s is the speed of light in vacuum.
Therefore, $n = \frac{3.0 \times 10^8}{2.0 \times 10^8} = 1.5$

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The ratio of speeds of electromagnetic waves in vacuum and a medium, having dielectric constant $k=3$ and permeability of $\mu=2 \mu_{0}$, is 
 ($\mu_{0}=$ permeability of vacuum)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $36: 1$
- **(B)** $3: 2$
- **(C)** $6: 1$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{6}: 1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{6}: 1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Speed of electromagnetic wave in vacuum: $c = \frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_0 \epsilon_0}}$
Speed in a medium with permeability $\mu = 2\mu_0$ and dielectric constant $k = 3$:
$v = \frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu \epsilon}} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mu_0 \times 3\epsilon_0}} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{6\mu_0\epsilon_0}}$
$v = \frac{1}{\sqrt{6}} \times \frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_0\epsilon_0}} = \frac{c}{\sqrt{6}}$
Ratio of speeds: $\frac{c}{v} = \sqrt{6}$
Therefore: $c : v = \sqrt{6} : 1$

</details>

---

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the electric field of an electromagnetic wave propagating through free space is given by : $E=\sqrt{377} \sin \left(6.27 \times 10^{3} t-2.09 \times 10^{-5} x\right) \mathrm{N} / \mathrm{C}$ 
 The average power of the electromagnetic wave is $\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}\right) \mathrm{W} / \mathrm{m}^{2}$. The value of $\alpha$ is $\_\_\_\_$ 
 (Take $\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{0}}{\varepsilon_{o}}}=377$ in SI units)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the electric field equation, amplitude:
$E_0 = \sqrt{377}$ N/C
Average intensity of EM wave:
$I_{avg} = \frac{E_0^2}{2Z_0}$ where $Z_0 = \sqrt{\mu_0/\varepsilon_0} = 377$ Ω
$I_{avg} = \frac{(\sqrt{377})^2}{2 \times 377} = \frac{377}{2 \times 377} = \frac{1}{2}$ W/m²
Given $I_{avg} = \frac{1}{\alpha}$ W/m², we have $\frac{1}{\alpha} = \frac{1}{2}$
Therefore, $\alpha = 2$

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Match List - I with List - II. 
 $\begin{array}{l} \text{List - I} & \text{List - II} \\ \text{Relation} & \text{Law} \\ \text{A. } \oint \vec{E} \cdot \overrightarrow{d l}=-\frac{d}{d t} \oint \vec{B} \cdot \overrightarrow{d a} & \text{I. Ampere's circuital law} \\ \text{B. } \oint \vec{B} \cdot \overrightarrow{d l}=\mu_{0}\left(I+\epsilon_{0} \frac{d \phi_{E}}{d t}\right) & \text{II. Faraday's laws of} \\ & \text{electromagnetic induction} \\ \text{C. } \oint \vec{E} \cdot \overrightarrow{d a}=\frac{1}{\epsilon_{0}} \int_{\mathrm{V}} \rho \mathrm{dv} & \text{III. Ampere - Maxwell law} \\ \text{D. } \oint \vec{B} \cdot \overrightarrow{d l}=\mu_{0} I & \text{IV. Gauss's law of electrostatics} \end{array}$ 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-II, B-III, C-I, D-IV
- **(B)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I
- **(C)** A-I, B-IV, C-III, D-II
- **(D)** A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I**

**Detailed Solution:**

Theoretical
A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I

A. $\oint \vec{E} \cdot d\vec{l} = -\frac{d}{dt} \oint \vec{B} \cdot d\vec{a}$ → Faraday's law of electromagnetic induction (II)
B. $\oint \vec{B} \cdot d\vec{l} = \mu_0 \left(I + \epsilon_0 \frac{d\Phi_E}{dt}\right)$ → Ampere-Maxwell law (III)
C. $\oint \vec{E} \cdot d\vec{a} = \frac{1}{\epsilon_0} \int \rho \, dv$ → Gauss's law of electrostatics (IV)
D. $\oint \vec{B} \cdot d\vec{l} = \mu_0 I$ → Ampere's circuital law (I)

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A laser beam has intensity of $4.0 \times 10^{14} \mathrm{~W} / \mathrm{m}^{2}$. The amplitude of magnetic field associated with beam is $\_\_\_\_$ T. 
 (Take $\epsilon_{\mathrm{o}}=8.85 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{C}^{2} / \mathrm{Nm}^{2}$ and $\mathrm{c}=3 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 18.3
- **(B)** 1.83
- **(C)** 5.5
- **(D)** 2.0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1.83**

**Detailed Solution:**

The intensity of an electromagnetic wave is
$I = \frac{1}{2} \epsilon_0 c E_0^2$.
From the relation $E_0 = c B_0$, we get $I = \frac{1}{2} \epsilon_0 c^3 B_0^2$.
Solving for magnetic field amplitude:
$B_0 = \sqrt{\frac{2I}{\epsilon_0 c^3}} = \sqrt{\frac{2 \times 4.0 \times 10^{14}}{8.85 \times 10^{-12} \times (3 \times 10^8)^3}} = \sqrt{\frac{8.0 \times 10^{14}}{2.39 \times 10^{14}}}$
$= \sqrt{3.347} = 1.83$ T.

</details>

---

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field of a plane electromagnetic wave, travelling in an unknown nonmagnetic medium is given by, $E_{\mathrm{y}}=20 \sin \left(3 \times 10^{6} x-4.5 \times 10^{14} \mathrm{t}\right) \mathrm{V} / \mathrm{m}$ (where $x, \mathrm{t}$ and other values have S.I. units). The dielectric constant of the medium is $\_\_\_\_$ (speed of light in free space is $3 \times 10^{8} \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the wave equation $E_y = 20\sin(3 \times 10^6 x - 4.5 \times 10^{14} t)$:
Wave number: $k = 3 \times 10^6$ m⁻¹
Angular frequency: $\omega = 4.5 \times 10^{14}$ rad/s
Speed of wave in medium: $v = \frac{\omega}{k} = \frac{4.5 \times 10^{14}}{3 \times 10^6} = 1.5 \times 10^8$ m/s
Speed of light in vacuum: $c = 3 \times 10^8$ m/s
Refractive index: $n = \frac{c}{v} = \frac{3 \times 10^8}{1.5 \times 10^8} = 2$
For a nonmagnetic medium: $n = \sqrt{\epsilon_r}$
Dielectric constant: $\epsilon_r = n^2 = 4$

</details>

---

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An electromagnetic wave of frequency 100 MHz propagates through a medium of conductivity, $\sigma=10 \mathrm{mho} / \mathrm{m}$. The ratio of maximum conduction current density to maximum displacement current density is $\_\_\_\_$. 
 $\left[\text{Take }\frac{1}{4 \pi \epsilon_{\mathrm{o}}}=9 \times 10^{9} \mathrm{Nm}^{2} / \mathrm{C}^{2}\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1800**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an electromagnetic wave in a conducting medium, the maximum conduction current density is $J_{c,max} = \sigma E_0$ and maximum displacement current density is $J_{d,max} = \epsilon_0 \omega E_0$.
The ratio is: $\frac{J_{c,max}}{J_{d,max}} = \frac{\sigma}{\epsilon_0 \omega}$.
With $\sigma = 10$ mho/m, $f = 100$ MHz
So $\omega = 2\pi \times 10^8$ rad/s, and $\epsilon_0 = \frac{10^{-9}}{36\pi}$ F/m: $\frac{J_{c,max}}{J_{d,max}} = \frac{10 \times 36\pi}{10^{-9} \times 2\pi \times 10^8} = \frac{10 \times 36}{2 \times 10^{-1}} = 1800$.

</details>

---

### Q15. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field in a plane electromagnetic wave is given by : 
 $E_{y}=69 \sin \left[0.6 \times 10^{3} x-1.8 \times 10^{11} t\right] \mathrm{V} / \mathrm{m}$. 
 The expression for magnetic field associated with this electromagnetic wave is $\_\_\_\_$ T.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $B_{z}=2.3 \times 10^{-7} \sin \left[0.6 \times 10^{3} x-1.8 \times 10^{11} t\right]$
- **(B)** $B_{z}=2.3 \times 10^{-7} \sin \left[0.6 \times 10^{3} x+1.8 \times 10^{11} t\right]$
- **(C)** $B_{y}=69 \sin \left[0.6 \times 10^{3} x+1.8 \times 10^{11} t\right]$
- **(D)** $B_{y}=2.3 \times 10^{-7} \sin \left[0.6 \times 10^{3} x-1.8 \times 10^{11} t\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $B_{z}=2.3 \times 10^{-7} \sin \left[0.6 \times 10^{3} x-1.8 \times 10^{11} t\right]$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $E_y = 69 \sin[0.6 \times 10^3 x - 1.8 \times 10^{11} t]$ V/m
Wave propagates along +x (form $kx - \omega t$), $\vec{E}$ is along y.
For EM wave: $\vec{E} \times \vec{B}$ = direction of propagation
$\hat{j} \times \hat{k} = \hat{i}$, so $\vec{B}$ is along z-direction.
$B_0 = \frac{E_0}{c} = \frac{69}{3 \times 10^8} = 2.3 \times 10^{-7}$ T
E and B are in phase for plane EM wave.
$B_z = 2.3 \times 10^{-7} \sin[0.6 \times 10^3 x - 1.8 \times 10^{11} t]$ T

</details>

(I = intensity of an electromagnetic wave, $\mathrm{c}:$ speed of light)

**Options:**

- **(A)** Vm
- **(B)** NC
- **(C)** Nm

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two plane polarized light waves combine at a certain point whose electric field components are
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{E}_1=\mathrm{E}_0 \sin \omega \mathrm{t} \\ & \mathrm{E}_2=\mathrm{E}_0 \sin \left(\omega \mathrm{t}+\frac{\pi}{3}\right)\end{aligned}$
Find the amplitude of the resultant wave.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.9 E
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E}_0$
- **(C)** $1.7 \mathrm{E}_0$
- **(D)** $3.4 \mathrm{E}_0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.7 \mathrm{E}_0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{E}=\sqrt{\left(\mathrm{E}_0\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{E}_0\right)^2+2\left(\mathrm{E}_0\right)\left(\mathrm{E}_0\right) \cos \frac{\pi}{3}}$
$\mathrm{E}=\sqrt{2 \mathrm{E}_0^2+\mathrm{E}_0^2}=\sqrt{3} \mathrm{E}_0=1.73 \mathrm{E}_0$

</details>

---

### Q18. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

If an optical medium possesses a relative permeability of $\frac{10}{\pi}$ and relative permittivity of $\frac{1}{0.0885}$, then the velocity of light is greater in vacuum than that in this medium by ________ times.
$\left(\mu_0=4 \pi \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{H} / \mathrm{m}, \epsilon_0=8.85 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{~F} / \mathrm{m}\right.$
$\left.\mathrm{c}=3 \times 10^8 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since velocity of light in terms of $\mu \& E$ is
$\begin{aligned}
& V=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu \epsilon}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_0 \mu_r}} \times \frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon_0 \epsilon_r}} \\
& =\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{\mathrm{r}} \epsilon_{\mathrm{r}}}} \times \frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_0 \epsilon_0}} \\
& =\frac{C}{\sqrt{\mu_{\mathrm{r}} \epsilon_{\mathrm{r}}}}=\frac{C}{\sqrt{\frac{10}{\pi} \times \frac{1}{0.0885}}} \\
& =\frac{C}{\sqrt{36}}=\frac{C}{6}
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{V} & =\frac{\mathrm{C}}{6} \\
\mathrm{C} & =6 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$
Velocity of light in vacuum is greater by 6 times the velocity of light in medium
Answer is 6

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The magnetic field of an E.M. wave is given by $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \hat{i}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{j}\right) 30 \sin \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}-\frac{z}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]$ (S.I. Units).
The corresponding electric field in S.I. units is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\left(\frac{1}{2} \hat{i}-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \hat{j}\right) 30 \mathrm{c} \sin \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}-\frac{z}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]$
- **(B)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\left(\frac{3}{4} \hat{i}+\frac{1}{4} \hat{j}\right) 30 \mathrm{c} \cos \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}-\frac{z}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]$
- **(C)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\left(\frac{1}{2} \hat{i}+\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \hat{j}\right) 30 \mathrm{c} \sin \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}+\frac{z}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]$
- **(D)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \hat{i}-\frac{1}{2} \hat{j}\right) 30 \mathrm{c} \sin \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}+\frac{z}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\left(\frac{1}{2} \hat{i}-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \hat{j}\right) 30 \mathrm{c} \sin \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}-\frac{z}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{j}}\right) 30 \sin \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}-\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right] \\
& \overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}} \times \overrightarrow{\mathrm{c}} \text { and } \mathrm{E}=\mathrm{B}_0 \mathrm{c}
\end{aligned}$

Here $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}(-\hat{\mathrm{j}})+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{i}}\right)$
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_0=30 \mathrm{c} \\
& \overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\left(\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \hat{\mathrm{j}}\right) 30 \mathrm{c} \sin \left[\omega\left(\mathrm{t}-\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Due to presence of an em-wave whose electric component is given by $\mathrm{E}=100 \sin (\omega \mathrm{t}-\mathrm{kx}) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$, a cylinder of length 200 cm holds certain amount of em-energy inside it. If another cylinder of same length but half diameter than previous one holds same amount of em-energy, the magnitude of the electric field of the corresponding em-wave should be modified as

**Options:**

- **(A)** $400 \sin (\omega \mathrm{t}-\mathrm{kx}) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $200 \sin (\omega t-k x) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $50 \sin (\omega \mathrm{t}-\mathrm{kx}) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $25 \sin (\omega \mathrm{t}-\mathrm{kx}) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $200 \sin (\omega t-k x) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Energy density of an $E M_{\text {wave }}=\frac{1}{2} \varepsilon E_0^2$, whare $E_0$ is the amplitude of the wave.
Since total energy is same for both cylinders
$\begin{aligned}
& \left(\frac{1}{2} \varepsilon E_1^2\right) \pi R_1^2 L_1=\left(\frac{1}{2} \varepsilon E_2^2\right) \pi R_2^2 L_2 \\
& \Rightarrow E_1^2 R_1^2 L_1=E_2^2 R_2^2 L_2 \\
& \text { or } E_2=\frac{E_1 R_1}{R_2} \sqrt{\frac{L_1}{L_2}}=\frac{100 \mathrm{~d}}{(\mathrm{~d} / 2)} \sqrt{\frac{L_1}{L 1}}=200 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C} \\
& \qquad \quad\left[\because L_1=L_2=200 \mathrm{~cm}\right]
\end{aligned}$
$\Rightarrow$ The amplitude of corresponding $E M$ wave is 200 N/C
or the wave is $E=200 \sin (\omega t-k x) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane electromagnetic wave of frequency 20 MHz travels in free space along the $+x$ direction. At a particular point in space and time, the electric field vector of the wave is $\mathrm{E}_y=9.3 \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$. Then, the magnetic field vector of the wave at that point is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{B}_z=6.2 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~T}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{B}_z=3.1 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~T}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{B}_z=1.55 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~T}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{B}_z=9.3 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~T}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{B}_z=3.1 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{E}=\mathrm{BC} \\ & 9.3=\mathrm{B} \times 3 \times 10^8 \\ & \mathrm{~B}=\frac{9.3}{3 \times 10^8}=3.1 \times 10^{-8} \mathrm{~T}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q24. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field of an electromagnetic wave in free space is $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=57 \cos \left[7.5 \times 10^6 \mathrm{t}-5 \times 10^{-3}(3 x+4 y)\right](4 \hat{i}-3 \hat{j}) N / C$.
The associated magnetic field in Tesla is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\stackrel{\rightharpoonup}{\mathrm{B}}=\frac{57}{3 \times 10^8} \cos \left[7.5 \times 10^6 \mathrm{t}-5 \times 10^{-3}(3 x+4 y)\right](\hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=-\frac{57}{3 \times 10^8} \cos \left[7.5 \times 10^6 \mathrm{t}-5 \times 10^{-3}(3 x+4 y)\right](\hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=-\frac{57}{3 \times 10^8} \cos \left[7.5 \times 10^6 \mathrm{t}-5 \times 10^{-3}(3 x+4 y)\right](5 \hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=\frac{57}{3 \times 10^8} \cos \left[7.5 \times 10^6 \mathrm{t}-5 \times 10^{-3}(3 x+4 y)\right](5 \hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=-\frac{57}{3 \times 10^8} \cos \left[7.5 \times 10^6 \mathrm{t}-5 \times 10^{-3}(3 x+4 y)\right](5 \hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \overrightarrow{\mathrm{K}}=3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{j}} \\ & \hat{\mathrm{K}}=\frac{3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}}{5} \\ & \hat{\mathrm{E}}=\frac{4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}}{5} \\ & \hat{\mathrm{~B}}=\hat{\mathrm{K}} \times \hat{\mathrm{E}} \\ & \hat{\mathrm{B}}=-\hat{\mathrm{Z}} \\ & \mathrm{B}_0=\frac{\mathrm{E}_0}{\mathrm{C}}=\frac{57}{3 \times 10^8}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A parallel plate capacitor of area $A=16 \mathrm{~cm}^2$ and separation between the plates 10 cm , is charged by a DC current. Consider a hypothetical plane surface of area $\mathrm{A}_0=3.2 \mathrm{~cm}^2$ inside the capacitor and parallel to the plates. At an instant, the current through the circuit is 6A. At the same instant the displacement current through $\mathrm{A}_0$ is ________ mA .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1200**

**Detailed Solution:**

$i_d=i_c$
$\therefore$ Total displacement current $=6 \mathrm{~A}$
Through $A_0$
$\begin{aligned}
& i=\left(\frac{A_0}{A}\right) 6 \\
& =\frac{3.2}{16} \times 6=1.2 \mathrm{~A}=1200 \mathrm{~mA}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The magnetic field in a plane electromagnetic wave is $\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{y}}=\left(3.5 \times 10^{-7}\right) \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 x+0.5 \times 10^{11} t\right) \mathrm{T}$. The corresponding electric field will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $E_y=10.5 \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 x+0.5 \times 10^{11} t\right) \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $E_z=1.17 \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 x+0.5 \times 10^{11} t\right) \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $E_y=1.17 \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 x+0.5 \times 10^{11} t\right) \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $E_z=105 \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 x+0.5 \times 10^{11} t\right) \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $E_z=105 \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 x+0.5 \times 10^{11} t\right) \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}& \mathrm{E}_0=\mathrm{B}_0 \mathrm{C} \\& \mathrm{E}_0=3 \times 10^8 \times\left(3.5 \times 10^{-7}\right) \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 \mathrm{x}+0.5 \times 10^{11} \mathrm{t}\right) \\& \mathrm{E}_0=105 \sin \left(1.5 \times 10^3 \mathrm{x}+0.5 \times 10^{11} \mathrm{t}\right) \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}
\end{aligned}$
Data inconsistent while calculating speed of wave. You can challenge for data.

### Q27. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane EM wave is propagating along $x$ direction. It has a wavelength of $4 \mathrm{~mm}$. If electric field is in $y$ direction with the maximum magnitude of $60 \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$, the equation for magnetic field is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{B}_z=2 \times 10^{-7} \sin \left[\frac{\pi}{2} \times 10^3\left(x-3 \times 10^8 \mathrm{t}\right)\right] \hat{\mathrm{k}} \mathrm{T}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{B}_z=60 \sin \left[\frac{\pi}{2}\left(x-3 \times 10^8 \mathrm{t}\right)\right] \hat{\mathrm{k}} \mathrm{T}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{B}_x=60 \sin \left[\frac{\pi}{2}\left(x-3 \times 10^8 \mathrm{t}\right)\right] \hat{\mathrm{i} ~ T}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{B}_z=2 \times 10^{-7} \sin \left[\frac{\pi}{2}\left(x-3 \times 10^8 \mathrm{t}\right)\right] \hat{\mathrm{k}} \mathrm{T}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{B}_z=2 \times 10^{-7} \sin \left[\frac{\pi}{2} \times 10^3\left(x-3 \times 10^8 \mathrm{t}\right)\right] \hat{\mathrm{k}} \mathrm{T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{E}=\mathrm{BC} \Rightarrow 60=\mathrm{B} \times 3 \times 10^8$
$\Rightarrow \mathrm{B}=2 \times 10^{-7}$
Also $\quad \mathrm{C}=\mathrm{f} \lambda$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow 3 \times 10^8=\mathrm{f} \times 4 \times 10^{-3} \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{f}=\frac{3}{4} \times 10^{11} \\ & \Rightarrow \omega=2 \pi \mathrm{f}=\frac{3}{4} \times 2 \pi \times 10^{11} \\ & \Rightarrow \omega=\frac{\pi}{2} \times 10^3 \mathrm{C}\end{aligned}$
$\Rightarrow \quad$ Electric field $\Rightarrow y$ direction
Propagation $\Rightarrow \mathrm{x}$ direction
Magnetic field $\Rightarrow \mathrm{z}$-direction

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In the given electromagnetic wave $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{y}}=600 \sin (\omega \mathrm{t}-\mathrm{kx}) \mathrm{Vm}^{-1}$, intensity of the associated light beam is (in $\mathrm{W} / \mathrm{m}^2$ : (Given $\epsilon_0=9 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{C}^2 \mathrm{~N}^{-1} \mathrm{~m}^{-2}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 243
- **(B)** 729
- **(C)** 972
- **(D)** 486

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 486**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \text { Intensity } & =\frac{1}{2} \varepsilon_0 \mathrm{E}_0^2 \mathrm{c} \\ & =\frac{1}{2} \times 9 \times 10^{-12} \times(600)^2 \times 3 \times 10^8 \\ & =\frac{9}{2} \times 36 \times 3=486 \mathrm{w} / \mathrm{m}^2\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Electromagnetic waves travel in a medium with speed of $1.5 \times 10^8 \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~s}^{-1}$. The relative permeability of the medium is 2.0 . The relative permittivity will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 5
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{m}} \times \mu_{\mathrm{m}}}{\varepsilon_0 \times \mu_0}=\frac{\frac{1}{\mathrm{v}^2}}{\frac{1}{\mathrm{c}^2}} \\ & \varepsilon_{\mathrm{r}} \times \mu_{\mathrm{r}}=\frac{\mathrm{c}^2}{\mathrm{v}^2} \\ & \varepsilon_{\mathrm{r}} \times 2=\frac{\left(3 \times 10^8\right)^2}{\left(1.5 \times 10^8\right)^2} \\ & \varepsilon_{\mathrm{r}} \times 2=4 \\ & \varepsilon_{\mathrm{r}}=2\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An alternating voltage of amplitude $40 \mathrm{~V}$ and frequency $4 \mathrm{kHz}$ is applied directly across the capacitor of $12 \mu \mathrm{F}$. The maximum displacement current between the plates of the capacitor is nearly :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $10 \mathrm{~A}$
- **(B)** $12 \mathrm{~A}$
- **(C)** $8 \mathrm{~A}$
- **(D)** $13 \mathrm{~A}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $12 \mathrm{~A}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Displacement current is same as conduction current in capacitor.
$\begin{aligned}
& X_C=\frac{1}{\omega \mathrm{C}}=\frac{1}{2 \pi \mathrm{fC}} \\
& =\frac{1}{2 \pi \times 4 \times 10^3 \times 12 \times 10^{-6}}=3.317 \Omega
\end{aligned}$
$I=\frac{V}{X_C}=\frac{40}{3.317}=12 \mathrm{~A}$

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field in an electromagnetic wave is given by $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\hat{i} 40 \cos \omega(\mathrm{t}-z / \mathrm{c}) \mathrm{NC}^{-1}$. The magnetic field induction of this wave is (in SI unit) :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=\hat{k} \frac{40}{\mathrm{C}} \cos \omega(\mathrm{t}-z / \mathrm{c})$
- **(B)** $\vec{B}=\hat{j} 40 \cos \omega(t-z / c)$
- **(C)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=\hat{i} \frac{40}{\mathrm{C}} \cos \omega(\mathrm{t}-z / \mathrm{c})$
- **(D)** $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=\hat{j} \frac{40}{\mathrm{C}} \cos \omega(\mathrm{t}-z / \mathrm{c})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}=\hat{j} \frac{40}{\mathrm{C}} \cos \omega(\mathrm{t}-z / \mathrm{c})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}} 40 \cos \omega\left(\mathrm{t}-\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{c}}\right)$
$\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}}$ is along $+\mathrm{x}$ direction
$\overrightarrow{\mathrm{v}}$ is along $+\mathrm{z}$ direction
So direction of $\vec{B}$ will be along +y and magnitude of $B$ will be $\frac{E}{c}$
So answer is $\frac{40}{c} \cos \omega\left(t-\frac{z}{c}\right) \hat{j}$

</details>

The total average energy density of the electromagnetic field of the wave is : [Use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ε</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>106</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>425</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>212</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>212</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>106</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Total average energy density of the electromagnetic field is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>ε</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>50</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>106</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Match List I with List II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;"> <tbody> <tr> <td> </td> <td>List I</td> <td> </td> <td>List II</td> </tr> <tr> <td>A.</td> <td>Gauss’s law of magnetostatics</td> <td>i.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>ε</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>∫</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>B.</td> <td>Faraday’s law of electro magnetic induction</td> <td>ii.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>C.</td> <td>Ampere’s law</td> <td>iii.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>∫</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>→</mo></mover></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>D.</td> <td>Gauss’s law of electrostatics</td> <td>iv.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></math></td> </tr> </tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-I, B-III, C-IV, D-II
- **(B)** A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II
- **(C)** A-IV, B-II, C-III, D-I
- **(D)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Gauss’s law of magnetostatics:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Faraday’s law of electro magnetic induction:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>∫</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>→</mo></mover></math></p><p>Ampere’s law:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></math></p><p>Gauss’s law of electrostatics:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∮</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>·</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>ε</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>∫</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q36. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The electric field of an electromagnetic wave in free space is represented as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>. The corresponding magnetic induction vector will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>C</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>C</mi></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>C</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>C</mi></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>C</mi></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>:</p><p>Clearly, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo></math> term represents, electromagnetic wave is travelling along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi></math> direction or along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>.</p><p>Now, in vector form, direction of electromagnetive wave is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>C</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>×</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>. Therefore, magnetic field is along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>. </p><p>Also, we know that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>C</mi></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> is speed of light.</p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>C</mi></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>.<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q37. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A plane electromagnetic wave of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>35</mn><mo> </mo><mi>MHz</mi></math> travels in free space along the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math>-direction. At a particular point (in space and time) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The value of magnetic field at this point is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>At every instant, the ratio of the magnitude of the electric field to the magnitude of the magnetic field in an electromagnetic wave equals the speed of light.</p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>B</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>B</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p>Now, direction of magnetic field can be calculated as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>×</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>×</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q38. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An object is placed in a medium of refractive index <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>. An electromagnetic wave of intensity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> falls normally on the object and it is absorbed completely. The radiation pressure on the object would be (speed of light in free space <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ):

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>36</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The formula to calculate the radiation pressure is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>I</mi><mi>v</mi></mfrac><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Also, the velocity of the electromagnetic wave can be expressed as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mi>v</mi></mfrac><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Equations (1) and (2) imply that</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mi>P</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>·</mo><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q39. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A plane electromagnetic wave propagating in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-direction is described by
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>200</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin</mi><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>;</mo><mo> </mo></math>The intensity of the wave is :
(Use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ϵ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>35</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>53</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>26</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>106</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>53</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The magnitude of the electric field component of the electromagnetic wave is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>The formula to calculate the intensity of the electromagnetic wave is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>ε</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">0</mn><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn></msubsup><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>From equation (1), it follows that</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mi>I</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>×</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>200</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>53</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2023 (15 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Match List-I with List II of Electromagnetic waves with corresponding wavelength range:</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td>List I</td>			<td> </td>			<td>List II</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(A)</td>			<td>Microwave</td>			<td>(I)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(B)</td>			<td>Ultraviolet</td>			<td>(II)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(C)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math>-Ray</td>			<td>(III)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mm</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>700</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(D)</td>			<td>Infra-red</td>			<td>(IV)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mm</mi></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The electromagnetic spectrum is the range of all types of EM radiation. Radiation is energy that travels and spreads out with different energies. In the daily life, two commonly known EM waves are the light wave and the radio wave.</p><p>The other types of EM radiation that make up the electromagnetic spectrum are microwaves, infrared light, ultraviolet light, X-rays and gamma-rays.</p><p>The wavelengths of different parts of the electromagnetic spectrum are related by the following relation:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>UV</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>IR</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>MW</mi></msub></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an electromagnetic wave, at an instant and at a particular position, the electric field is along the negative z-axis and magnetic field is along the positive x-axis. Then the direction of propagation of electromagnetic wave is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** positive z-axis
- **(B)** positive y-axis
- **(C)** at  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>angle from positive  y -axis
- **(D)** negative y-axis

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) negative y-axis**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Direction of propagation of the electromagnetic wave is along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><mi>E</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math></p><p>Thus,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>E</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mfenced></math></p><p>Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements:</p><p>Statement I : Out of microwaves, infrared rays and ultraviolet rays, ultraviolet rays are the most effective for the emission of electrons from a metallic surface</p><p>Statement II : Above the threshold frequency, the maximum kinetic energy of photoelectrons is inversely proportional to the frequency of the incident light</p><p>In the light of above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below</p><p> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(B)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Statement I is true but Statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Out of the three types of radiation, UV rays would be having highest frequency. The maximum kinetic energy is </p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p>Clearly, the maximum kinetic energy is dependant on the frequency of the incident radiation and the work function of the metal.</p><p>From the given spectrums UV rays have the highest energy. Hence, this option is the correct one.</p><p> </p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Which of the following Maxwell&rsquo;s equation is valid for time varying conditions but not valid for static conditions:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>D</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>Q</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Maxwell&rsquo;s four equations describe the electric and magnetic fields arising from distributions of electric charges and currents, and how those fields change in time.</p><p>The equations are as follows:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>d</mi><mover><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>q</mi><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8640;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>d</mi><mover><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8640;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8640;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8640;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8706;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8640;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8640;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>From the above set of equations, it is clear that only the third equation is valid for time varying field.</p><p>Hence, this is the correct option.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Given below are two statements: one is labelled as <strong>Assertion A</strong> and the other is labelled as <strong>Reason R</strong></p><p><strong>Assertion A :</strong> EM waves used for optical communication have longer wavelengths than that of microwave, employed in Radar technology.</p><p><strong>Reason R :</strong> Infrared EM waves are more energetic than microwaves, (used in Radar)</p><p>In the light of given statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A
- **(B)** A is false but R is true
- **(C)** A is true but R is false
- **(D)** Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A is false but R is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>An optical telecommunication is communication at a distance using light to carry information. It can be performed visually or by using electronic devices.</p><p>The wavelength of the microwaves are greater than that of the common optical light waves.</p><p>Hence, the Assertion is not correct. But, because of the small wavelength, the electromagnetic waves are more energetic than the microwaves. Hence, the reason is true.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A plane electromagnetic wave of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>MHz</mi></math> propagates in free space along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-direction. At a particular space and time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. What is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> at this point?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>It is given that </p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>.</p><p>The relation between the electric, magnetic field and speed of light is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>c</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/df1fb381-b64c-4835-97fb-e590b1b7f9cc.png" style="width: 328px; height: 246px;" />
Clearly,   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Using the relation,
     <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>E</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>
