### Q46. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The energy density associated with electric field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> and magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> of an electromagnetic wave in free space is given by (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo></math> permittivity of free space, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo></math> permeability of free space)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>When an electromagnetic wave propagates from the source, it transfers energy to the objects in its path. Electric and magnetic fields serve as energy reservoirs for electromagnetic waves. An electromagnetic wave contains the same amount of energy as are contained in the electric and magnetic fields together. In such situation, the energy density of the electromagnetic wave is equal to the sum of the energies of the electric and magnetic fields.</p><p>The average electric and magnetic energy densities are given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>E</mi></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q47. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The ratio of average electric energy density and total average energy density of electromagnetic wave is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Average electric energy density is stated as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>u</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Total average energy density <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>    (As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac></math>)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Hence, the ratio, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>u</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mi>U</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p><strong>Statement I :</strong> Electromagnetic waves are not deflected by electric and magnetic field.</p><p><strong>Statement II :</strong> The amplitude of electric field and the magnetic field in electromagnetic waves are related to each other as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></msqrt><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is true but statement II is false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(C)** Statement I is false but statement II is true
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Statement I is true but statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Statement-I is correct as Electromagnetic waves are neutral.</p><p>Statement-II is wrong as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Which of the following are true?</p><p>A. Speed of light in vacuum is dependent on the direction of propagation.</p><p>B. Speed of light in a medium is independent of the wavelength of light.</p><p>C. The speed of light is independent of the motion of the source.</p><p>D. The speed of light in a medium is independent of intensity.</p><p>Choose the correct answer from the question given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and C only
- **(B)** B and D only
- **(C)** B and C only
- **(D)** C and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) C and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Speed of light in vaccum is independent of the frequency, wavelength, nature of the source, direction of propagation, motion of the source and intensity of the wave. But it depends on the refractive index of the medium.</p><p>Therefore, the speed of light in vacuum is a universal constant.</p><p>Hence, C and D are only correct.</p>

</details>

---

### Q50. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Match List I and List II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 600px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td>A</td>			<td>Gauss&rsquo;s Law in Electrostatics  </td>			<td>I</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mover><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B</td>			<td>Faraday&#39;s Law</td>			<td>II</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> </td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td>Gauss&rsquo;s Law in Magnetism</td>			<td>III</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>E</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td>Ampere-Maxwell Law</td>			<td>IV</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>q</mi><msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III
- **(B)** A-I, B-II, C-III, D-IV
- **(C)** A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II
- **(D)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(A) Gauss law states that the total electric flux out of a closed surface is equal to the charge enclosed divided by the permittivity. So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>q</mi><msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p><p>(B) Faraday&rsquo;s law explains that the direction of the current will be opposite to the direction of flux that produced it, so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>(C) Gauss&#39;s law for magnetism states that the magnetic flux B across any closed surface is zero, so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>(D) Ampere&rsquo;s Maxwell law is stated as </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>E</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math>Conduction current and </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>E</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math>Displacement current</p><p>Thus, A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III</p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

All electromagnetic wave is transporting energy in the negative <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> direction. At a certain point and certain time the direction of electric field of the wave is along positive <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math> direction. What will be the direction of the magnetic field of the wave at that point and instant?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Positive direction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>
- **(B)** Positive direction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>
- **(C)** Negative direction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>
- **(D)** Negative direction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Positive direction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In an electromagnetic wave, the direction of energy flow is along the direction of the wave propagation. Therefore, the wave propagates along negative z direction. </p><p>Electric field is along the positive y direction and the wave propagates along negative z direction. Thus, magnetic field is in direction perpendicular to both z and y axes. </p><p>Using vector algebra, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, magnetic field is in positive <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> direction.</p>

</details>

---

### Q52. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The electric field and magnetic field components of an electromagnetic wave going through vacuum is described by</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Then the correct relation between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is given by </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>k</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we know, electric field and magnetic field are related as </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math> is speed of light in vacuum</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Also</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q53. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> represent electric field and propagation vectors of the EM waves in vacuum, then magnetic field vector is given by:</p><p>(<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo></math> angular frequency)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we know, direction of electromagnetic wave will be in the direction of the vector <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/df4f1736-6c80-421b-8a73-f12d1d9417c4-4df5bc56-865b-4289-a99c-42f8644df801-5eab399a-ea68-4e15-8547-bdfb5dd64804-9884755.png" style="width: 250px; height: 239px;" /></p><p>Magnitude of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>K</mi><mi>&#969;</mi></mfrac><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Sun light falls normally on a surface of area <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and exerts an average force of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> within a time period of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn></math> minutes. Considering a case of complete absorption, the energy flux of incident light is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>64</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The radiation pressure in case of complete absorption is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>I</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Therefore, the force will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>I</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>A</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>A</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>36</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>I</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Identify the correct statements from the following descriptions of various properties of electromagnetic waves.</p><p>A. In a plane electromagnetic wave electric field and magnetic field must be perpendicular to each other and direction of propagation of wave should be along electric field or magnetic field.</p><p>B. The energy in electromagnetic wave is divided equally between electric and magnetic fields.</p><p>C. Both electric field and magnetic field are parallel to each other and perpendicular to the direction of propagation of wave.</p><p>D. The electric field, magnetic field and direction of propagation of wave must be perpendicular to each other.</p><p>E. The ratio of amplitude of magnetic field to the amplitude of electric field is equal to speed of light.</p><p>Choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** D only
- **(B)** B and D only
- **(C)** B, C and E only
- **(D)** A, B and E only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) B and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>In an electromagnetic wave electric field magnetic field and the direction of propagation of the wave are mutually perpendicular. Therefore, statement <strong>D </strong>is correct and statement <strong>A </strong>and <strong>C </strong>are incorrect.</p><p>The energy is equally divided between electric and magnetic field. Therefore, statement <strong>B</strong> is correct.</p><p>Speed of light <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></math>. Therefore, statement <strong>E </strong>is incorrect.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A beam of light travelling along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math>-axis is described by the electric field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>900</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#969;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>. The ratio of electric force to magnetic force on a charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> moving along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math>-axis with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> will be :</p><p>[Given speed of light <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ]</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>10</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>900</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>900</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4908a3d2-9c62-4497-b650-2e3c0c09bf47-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 132px;" /></p><p>Electric field and electric force are related as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>q</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p>Magnetic force of a moving charge is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>q</mi><mi>v</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p>Thus, the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mi>v</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Using relation between magnetic and electric field, we have</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mi>v</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The oscillating magnetic field in a plane electromagnetic wave is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mn>1000</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. The amplitude of electric field will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>12</mn></msup><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mn>1000</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p>Here, peak value of magnetic field, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p>Now the speed of the wave <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1000</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1000</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Using relation between magnetic and electric field, </p><p>Peak value of electric field is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The magnetic field of a plane electromagnetic wave is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. The amplitude of the electric field would be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-axis
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>-axis
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>-axis
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>-axis

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>-axis**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The ratio of electric field amplitude to the magnetic field amplitude of an electromagnetic wave is equal to the speed of light in the medium. Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>According to the given equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> the wave travels in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> direction. As electric field and magnetic field vibrate perpendicular to the velocity of light, electric field can not be along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> axis.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Light wave travelling in air along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-direction is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>540</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Then, the peak value of magnetic field of wave will be (Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>54</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>54</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>540</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Here, peak value of electric field, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>540</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Using relation between magnetic and electric field, </p><p>Peak value of magnetic field is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>540</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The intensity of the light from a bulb incident on a surface is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The amplitude of the magnetic field in this light-wave is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p>(Given : Permittivity of vacuum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, speed of light in vacuum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **43**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The intensity is defined as energy per unit time per unit area. Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Energy density is energy per unit volume. Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Dividing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>I</mi><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>d</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>I</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>22</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>22</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Magnetic energy density will be half of total energy density, Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p>Now energy density in magnetic field is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>. Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mi>rms</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mi>rms</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>43</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An EM wave propagating in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-direction has a wavelength of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mm</mi></math>. The electric field vibrating <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-direction has maximum magnitude of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Choose the correct equations for electric and magnetic fields if the EM wave is propagating in vacuum :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>
- **(B)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>
- **(C)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>
- **(D)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Using the relation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math> is speed of light in vacuum.</p><p>After analysing each option, we get option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is the correct option</p><p>as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>60</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p><strong>Statement I:</strong> A time varying electric field is a source of changing magnetic field and vice-versa. Thus a disturbance in electric or magnetic field creates EM waves.</p><p><strong>Statement II:</strong> In a material medium, the EM wave travels with speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac></math>.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both statement I and statement II are true
- **(B)** Both statement I and statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement I is correct but statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>According to the Maxwell&rsquo;s EM theory, the EM waves propagation contains electric and magnetic field vibration in mutually perpendicular direction. Thus, the changing of electric field give rise to magnetic field.</p><p>Also, time varying magnetic field produces electric field. According to Faradays law, when magnetic flux changes in the region surrounded by conductor, it produces electric field (induced emf) in conductor. </p><p>In a material medium the EM wave travels with speed given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right left right left right left right left right left right left" columnspacing="0em 2em 0em 2em 0em 2em 0em 2em 0em 2em 0em" rowspacing="3pt"><mtr><mtd><mi>v</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>&#949;</mi></msqrt></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>v</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow></msqrt></mfrac></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Thus, statement I is correct but statement II is false.</p>

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If Electric field intensity of a uniform plane electro magnetic wave is given as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>301</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>452</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> Then, magnetic intensity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>H</mi></math> of this wave in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> will be [Given : Speed of light in vacuum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, Permeability of vacuum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>x</mi></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>x</mi></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>x</mi></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>x</mi></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi>x</mi></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The relation between magnetic field and electric field is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>And magnetic intensity is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>H</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>H</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mi>c</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Also, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> are perpendicular to each other.</p><p>Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>H</mi><mrow><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>301</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>H</mi><mrow><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>452</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Hence, C is the answer.</p>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The electromagnetic waves travel in a medium at a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The relative permeability of the medium is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. The relative permittivity of the medium will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>m</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msup><mtext>s</mtext><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

The speed of electromagnetic waves in a medium is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>&#949;</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>..</mo><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math>

where <math> <mi>&#956;</mi> </math> and <math> <mi>&#949;</mi> </math> are absolute permeability and absolute permittivity of the medium.
Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#949;</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub></math>
Then, we have, 

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">0</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>10</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>10</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>

</details>

---

### Q65. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The electric field in an electromagnetic wave is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#969;</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mfenced><msup><mi>NC</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Find the intensity of the wave if it is propagating along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-axis in the free space. (Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn><msup><mi>Wm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Wm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Wm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>56</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><msup><mi>Wm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Wm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#969;</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Comparing it with the general equation of electromagnetic wave, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>We have, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>So, the intensity will be  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>c</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>56</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q66. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An electric bulb is rated as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math>. What will be the peak magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> distance produced by the radiations coming from this bulb? Consider this bulb as a point source with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> efficiency.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>19</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>71</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>84</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>71</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Intensity of light is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>Power</mi><mi>Area</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>200</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>100</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>034</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Using the relation between magnetic field and intensity, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>We have, magnetic field as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>034</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>69</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>71</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q67. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A plane electromagnetic wave travels in a medium of relative permeability <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>61</mn></math> and relative permittivity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn></math>. If magnitude of magnetic intensity is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> at a point, what will be the approximate magnitude of electric field intensity at that point ?</p><p>(Given : Permeability of free space <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, speed of light in vacuum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The speed of light in a given medium is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Magnetic field is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>H</mi></math>.</p><p>Also, the relation between the magnetic field and electric field is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>v</mi><mi>B</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><msqrt><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>H</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><msqrt><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>61</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>
