### Q68. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>There are two infinitely long straight current-carrying conductors and they are held at right angles to each other so that their common ends meet at the origin as shown in the figure given below. The ratio of current in both conductors is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. The magnetic field at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/90babf89-cd02-40fb-bdec-78468dd7066d-image.png" style="width: 230px; height: 233px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;xy</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;xy</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;xy</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><msub><mi>&#952;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><msub><mi>&#952;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mrow><mi>y</mi><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mrow><mi>x</mi><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>x</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>y</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The magnetic field vector of an electromagnetic wave is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfrac><mrow><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> represents unit vector along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis respectively. At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>,</mo></math> two electric charges <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></math> coulomb and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></math> coulomb located at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> respectively, have the same velocity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>,</mo></math> (where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math> is the velocity of light ). The ratio of the force acting on charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mi>cos</mi><mfenced><mi>&#960;</mi></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mi>cos</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
Force on charged particle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mover><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>c</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>
Force on charged particle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>q</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mover><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>c</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field in an electromagnetic wave is given by
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></math>
The energy contained in a cylinder of volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> is _______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>.</mo></math>
(given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **500**

**Detailed Solution:**

Energy density <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
Energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo></math> volume
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>50</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>500</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup></math>

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A plane electromagnetic wave with a frequency of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>MHz</mi></math> travels in free space. At a particular point in space and time, the electric field is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The magnetic field at this point will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is,

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Recall the formula of the magnitude of the magnetic field in terms of the magnitude of the electric field, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>, here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math> is the speed of light in a vacuum.

</details>

---

### Q72. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The electric field in a plane electromagnetic wave is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>500</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>10</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The velocity of an electromagnetic wave in this medium is: (Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo></math> the speed of light in vacuum).</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>c</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>c</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>c</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The equation of electric field is given in the question, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>500</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>10</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> now compare it the equation of electric field in an electromagnetic wave, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>10</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, so the velocity of electromagnetic waves is, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>10</mn></msup><mn>50</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>c</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A light beam is described by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#969;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> An electron is allowed to move normal to the propagation of light beam with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. What is the maximum magnetic force exerted on the electron?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>21</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>800</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>q</mi><mi>v</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>800</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q74. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field in a plane electromagnetic wave is given by
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mi>cos</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>.
If this wave falls normally on a perfectly reflecting surface having an area of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. If the radiation pressure exerted by the E.M. wave on the surface during a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>min</mi></math> exposure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>9</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Find the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **354**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></math>
Radiation pressure
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mi>c</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>C</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>200</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>354</mn><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>9</mn></msup></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>S</mi><mi>o</mi><mo>,</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>354</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q75. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A linearly polarised electromagnetic wave in vacuum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>cos</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is incident normally on a perfectly reflecting wall at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi></math>. Choose the correct option.

**Options:**

- **(A)** The wavelength is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>.
- **(B)** The frequency of electromagnetic wave is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>54</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>.
- **(C)** The transmitted wave will be
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>cos</mi><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>.
- **(D)** The reflected wave will be
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>cos</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) The reflected wave will be
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>cos</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reflected wave will have direction opposite to incident wave.

</details>

### Q76. JEE Main 2021 (22 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Intensity of sunlight is observed as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>092</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Wm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> at a point in free space. What will be the peak value of magnetic field at that point ? <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>31</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>88</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mrow><mi>avg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#38;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>C</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>C</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

</details>

### Q77. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an electromagnetic wave, the electric field vector and magnetic field vector are given as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>, respectively. The direction of
propagation of electromagnetic wave is along:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Direction of propagation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>.

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A plane electromagnetic wave propagating along y-direction can have the following pair of electric field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math> and magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math> components.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>x</mi></msub></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>x</mi></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>x</mi></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>z</mi></msub></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>x</mi></msub></math>**

### Q79. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A plane electromagnetic wave of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>MHz</mi></math> is traveling in a vacuum along the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo></math>direction. At a particular point in space and time, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#183;</mo></math> (where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> is unit vector along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo></math>direction) What is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> at this point?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math>. (Direction of the wave) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8214;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q80. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> average force is exerted by a light wave on a non-reflecting surface of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></math> area during <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>min</mi></math> of time span, the energy flux of light just before it falls on the surface is _______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math>(Round off to the Nearest Integer), (Assume complete absorption and normal incidence conditions are there)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>A</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mn>30</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Red light differs from blue light as they have :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Different frequencies and different wavelengths
- **(B)** Different frequencies and same wavelengths
- **(C)** Same frequencies and same wavelengths
- **(D)** Same frequencies and different wavelengths

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Different frequencies and different wavelengths**

**Detailed Solution:**

Red light and blue light have different wavelength and different frequency.

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For an electromagnetic wave travelling in free space, the relation between average energy densities due to electric <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>e</mi></msub></mfenced></math> and magnetic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mfenced></math> fields is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo>&#8800;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>EMW</mi></math>, Average energy density due to electric <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>e</mi></msub></mfenced></math> and magnetic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>m</mi></msub></mfenced></math> fields is same.

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A plane electromagnetic wave of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>MHz</mi></math> is traveling in a vacuum along the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo></math>direction. At a particular point in space and time, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. The value of the electric field at this point is:(speed of light <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>,</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>,</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> are unit vectors along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> direction.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>24</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>24</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>24</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>EM</mi></math> wave is travelling towards <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>24</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A radiation is emitted by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> bulb and it generates an electric field and magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>, placed at a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. The efficiency of the bulb is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>%</mo></math>. The value of peak electric field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is <u>                </u> (Rounded-off to the nearest integer) [Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **137**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>avg</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>c</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1000</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>187</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>689</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>136</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>89</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>136</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>89</mn></math></p><p>Rounding off to nearest integer <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>137</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An electromagnetic wave of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>GHz</mi></math> enters a dielectric medium of relative electric permittivity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math> from vacuum. The wavelength of this wave in that medium will be _________ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **667**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi></math> in vacuum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mi>f</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>9</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math> in medium <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Where refractive index</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></msqrt></math></p><p>Assuming non-magnetic material <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>15</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>667</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Match List - I with List - II.<table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1">List-I</td>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1">List-II</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(a)</td>			<td>Source of microwave frequency</td>			<td>(i)</td>			<td>Radioactive decay of nucleus</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(b)</td>			<td>Source of infrared frequency</td>			<td>(ii)</td>			<td>Magnetron</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(c)</td>			<td>Source of Gamma Rays</td>			<td>(iii)</td>			<td>Inner shell electrons</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(d)</td>			<td>Source of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math>-rays</td>			<td>(iv)</td>			<td>Vibration of atoms and molecules</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td> </td>			<td>(v)</td>			<td>LASER</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td> </td>			<td>(vi)</td>			<td>RC circuit</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (a)-(ii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(vi), (d)-(iii)
- **(B)** (a)-(vi), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(v)
- **(C)** (a)-(vi), (b)-(v), (c)-(i), (d)-(iv)
- **(D)** (a)-(ii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) (a)-(ii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii)**

**Detailed Solution:**

(a) Source of microwave frequency is magnetron.
(b) Source of infrared frequency is vibration of atoms and molecules.
(c) Source of Gamma rays is radioactive decay of nucleus
(d) Source of X-rays inner shell electron transition.

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

An electromagnetic wave of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>GHz</mi></math>, is travelling in a medium whose relative electric permittivity and relative magnetic permeability both are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo></math> Its velocity in this medium is _______<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **15**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given : Frequency of wave <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>GHz</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>9</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math></p><p>Relative permittivity, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and Relative permeability, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> Since speed of light in a medium is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>1</mn></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><msqrt><mi>&#956;</mi></msqrt><mo>&#8712;</mo></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>1</mn></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></mstyle></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mi>f</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>C</mi><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> is speed of light is vacuum</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><msqrt><mn>4</mn></msqrt></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>2</mn></mstyle></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Ans. is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>For a plane electromagnetic wave, the magnetic field at a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> and time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math> is :</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.2</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.5</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.5</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>.</p><p>The instantaneous electric field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> corresponding to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> is :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mn>36</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>36</mn></math></p><p>As the light is propagating in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> direction.</p><p>&amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>|</mo><mo>|</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> should be in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> direction.</p><p>So electric field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>sin</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>36</mn><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>11</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>The electric field of a plane electromagnetic wave is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>. Its magnetic field will be given by</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi>cos</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>x</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>y</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

As in electromagnetic  wave electric field is always perpendicular to the magnetic field and both the fields oscillates perpendicularly to the direction pf propagation so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#8214;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>

</details>

---

### Q90. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The electric field of a plane electromagnetic wave propagating along the x direction in vacuum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>jcos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>kx</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>.</mo></math> The magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>,</mo></math> at the moment <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>kx</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#1013;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt></math></p><p>As the light is propagating in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> direction</p><p>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>|</mo><mo>|</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> should be in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> direction</p>

</details>
