### Q124. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An electromagnetic wave of frequency $1 \times 10^{14}$ hertz is propagating along $\mathrm{z}$-axis. The amplitude of electric field is $4 \mathrm{~V} / \mathrm{m}$. If $\varepsilon_0=8.8 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{C}^2 / \mathrm{N}-$ $\mathrm{m}^2$, then average energy density of electric field will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $35.2 \times 10^{-10} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$
- **(B)** $35.2 \times 10^{-11} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$
- **(C)** $35.2 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$
- **(D)** $35.2 \times 10^{-13} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $35.2 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given: Amplitude of electric field,
$$
E_0=4 \mathrm{v} / \mathrm{m}
$$
Absolute permitivity,
$$
\varepsilon_0=8.8 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{c}^2 / \mathrm{N}-\mathrm{m}^2
$$
Average energy density $u_E=$ ?
Applying formula,
Average energy density $u_E=\frac{1}{4} \varepsilon_0 E^2$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\Rightarrow \quad u_E=\frac{1}{4} \times 8.8 \times 10^{-12} \times(4)^2 \\
&=35.2 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q125. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

During the propagation of electromagnetic wave in a particular medium :-

**Options:**

- **(A)** Electric energy density is double of the magnetic energy density
- **(B)** Electric energy density is half of the magnetic energy density
- **(C)** Electric energy density is equal to the magnetic energy density
- **(D)** Both electric and magnetic energy densities are zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Electric energy density is equal to the magnetic energy density**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>U</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>elec</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>      ....(i)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>U</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>mag</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>     ....(ii)

<math> <mrow> <mtext>B</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>E</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>C</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> (for EMW)         ....(iii)

and <math> <mrow> <mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <msqrt> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mi>&#949;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> .....(iv)

<mo>&there4; devide eq<sup>n</sup> (i) by (ii) </mo>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><msub><mi>&#949;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><msup> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mfrac> <mi>E</mi> <mi>B</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <msub> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mi>&#949;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math></p><p>by using eq (iii) &amp; (iv)

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>C</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>C</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mi>U</mi><mi>V</mi></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q126. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Select the correct statement from the following :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Electromagnetic waves cannot travel in vacuum.
- **(B)** Electromagnetic waves are longitudinal waves.
- **(C)** Electromagnetic waves are produced by charges moving with uniform velocity.
- **(D)** Electromagnetic waves carry both energy and momentum as they propagate through space.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Electromagnetic waves carry both energy and momentum as they propagate through space.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Electromagnetic waves do not required any medium to propagate. They can travel in vacuum. They are transverse in nature like light. They carry both energy and momentum.
A changing electric field produces a changing magnetic field and vice-versa. Which gives rise to a transverse wave known as electromagnetic wave.

</details>

### Q129. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A radio transmitter transmits at $830 \mathrm{kHz}$. At a certain distance from the transmitter magnetic field has amplitude $4.82 \times 10^{-11} \mathrm{~T}$. The electric field and the wavelength are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.014 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C}, 36 \mathrm{~m}$
- **(B)** $0.14 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C}, 36 \mathrm{~m}$
- **(C)** $0.14 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C}, 360 \mathrm{~m}$
- **(D)** $0.014 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C}, 360 \mathrm{~m}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $0.014 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C}, 360 \mathrm{~m}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Frequency of EM wave $v=830 \mathrm{KHz}$ $=830 \times 10^3 \mathrm{~Hz}$.
Magnetic field, $B=4.82 \times 10^{-11} \mathrm{~T}$
As we know, frequency, $v=\frac{c}{\lambda}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
\text { or } \lambda & =\frac{c}{v}=\frac{3 \times 10^8}{830 \times 10^3} \\
\lambda & \simeq 360 \mathrm{~m} \\
\text { And, } E & =B C=4.82 \times 10^{-11} \times 3 \times 10^8 \\
& =0.014 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q130. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An electromagnetic wave with frequency $\omega$ and wavelength $\lambda$ travels in the $+y$ direction. Its magnetic field is along $+x$-axis. The vector equation for the associated electric field (of amplitude $E_0$ ) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{E}=-E_0 \cos \left(\omega t+\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} y\right) \hat{x}$
- **(B)** $\vec{E}=E_0 \cos \left(\omega t-\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} y\right) \hat{x}$
- **(C)** $\vec{E}=E_0 \cos \left(\omega t-\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} y\right) \hat{z}$
- **(D)** $\vec{E}=-E_0 \cos \left(\omega t+\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} y\right) \hat{z}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\vec{E}=E_0 \cos \left(\omega t-\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} y\right) \hat{z}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In an electromagnetic wave electric field and magnetic field are perpendicular to the direction of propagation of wave. The vector equation for the electric field is
$$
\vec{E}=E_0 \cos \left(\omega t-\frac{2 \pi}{\lambda} y\right) \hat{z}
$$

</details>

---

### Q131. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An electromagnetic wave in vacuum has the electric and magnetic fields $\vec{E}$ and $\vec{B}$, which are always perpendicular to each other. The direction of polarization is given by $\vec{X}$ and that of wave propagation by $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{k}}$. Then :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{X} \| \vec{B}$ and $\vec{k} \| \vec{B} \times \vec{E}$
- **(B)** $\vec{X} \| \vec{E}$ and $\vec{k} \| \vec{E} \times \vec{B}$
- **(C)** $\vec{X} \| \vec{B}$ and $\vec{k} \| \vec{E} \times \vec{B}$
- **(D)** $\vec{X} \| \vec{E}$ and $\vec{k} \| \vec{B} \times \vec{E}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\vec{X} \| \vec{B}$ and $\vec{k} \| \vec{E} \times \vec{B}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Direction of polarization is parallel to magnetic field,
$\therefore \quad \overrightarrow{\mathrm{X}} \| \overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}$
and direction of wave propagation is parallel to $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}} \times \overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}$
$\therefore \quad \overrightarrow{\mathrm{K}} \| \overrightarrow{\mathrm{E}} \times \overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}$

</details>

### Q132. JEE Main 2006

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The rms value of the electric field of the light coming from the Sun is $720 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{C}$. The average total energy density of the electromagnetic wave is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.3 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$
- **(B)** $4.58 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$
- **(C)** $6.37 \times 10^{-9} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$
- **(D)** $81.35 \times 10^{-12} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4.58 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$U_{a v}=\varepsilon_0 E_{\text {rs }}^2=4.58 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$
$E_{\text {rms }}=4.58 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{m}^3$

</details>

### Q133. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An electromagnetic wave of frequency $v=3.0 \mathrm{MHz}$ passes from vacuum into a dielectric medium with permittivity $\varepsilon=4.0$. Then

**Options:**

- **(A)** wavelength is doubled and the frequency remains unchanged
- **(B)** wavelength is doubled and frequency becomes half
- **(C)** wavelength is halved and frequency remains unchanged
- **(D)** wavelength and frequency both remain unchanged.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) wavelength is halved and frequency remains unchanged**

**Detailed Solution:**

Refractive index, $\mu=\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{\varepsilon_0}}=2$
Speed and wavelength of wave will becomes half, the frequency remaining unchanged (frequency of a wave depends on the source as due to refraction, it is assumed that the energy is conserved. hv remains the same)

</details>
